  5. Human directing the Loop? Rethinking automation through Chinese transnational platform expansion in Oceania
    Tingting Liu, Yiran Liu · pp. 1601-1621 · online 2026-06-15
    摘要

    Digital platforms are often portrayed as increasingly dependent on automation to optimise user engagement and streamline governance, a logic commonly captured by the notion of the ‘human-in-the-loop’. Emerging evidence from transnational platforms, however, points to a more hybrid operational model in which algorithmic systems are actively steered through context-sensitive human intervention. This article examines Bigo Live, a Chinese-owned livestreaming platform, as it expands into Oceania, specifically Australia, New Zealand (ANZ), and Papua New Guinea. Drawing on in-depth qualitative research, including interviews and participant observation conducted in Guangzhou and Sydney, the study demonstrates that Bigo’s internationalisation relies heavily on human-led adaptation to negotiate intercultural sensitivities, localise marketing strategies, and comply with highly varied content regulations. Unexpected developments, such as the platform’s unanticipated popularity in Papua New Guinea, reveal the limitations of automation and the continuing centrality of human labour in recalibrating platform operations. The article introduces the concept of ‘human-directing-the-loop’ to describe a mode of platform governance in which human labour does not simply validate algorithmic outputs, but actively shapes how automation is deployed across culturally, legally, and infrastructurally uneven transnational environments.

  6. Rohingya participatory media in protracted displacement: Counterpublic formation through calibrated participation
    Faheem Hussain, Sara N. Amin, Tanya Trussler · pp. 1622-1642 · online 2026-06-17
    摘要

    This article examines Voice of Palong (VoP), a participatory audio-visual media initiative co-produced by Rohingya refugees and the Bangladeshi host community in Cox’s Bazar camps. Based on focus groups and field observations from 2023 to 2025, the study analyzes how VoP operates not simply as a communication project but as a relational media infrastructure through which counterpublic formations are assembled under conditions of displacement, surveillance, and legal precarity. Participation is calibrated and situational, shaped by mobility restrictions, gendered risk, limited access to technological resources, and asymmetries between host and refugee participants. VoP functioned as a situated counterpublic in which members calibrate storytelling, visibility, and circulation in relation to infrastructural and regulatory constraints. Findings also show that relational processes of co-production and circulation enabled incremental social repair across refugee-host divides. By situating participatory refugee media within border and media infrastructures, this article argues that counterpublic formation in protracted displacement is materially contingent, emerging through sustained negotiation with uneven conditions of access and control.

  7. Negotiating historical television in an era of political fragmentation and austerity: Tactics of heritage practitioners in Flanders, Belgium
    Sofie Vermoesen · pp. 1643-1661 · online 2026-06-22
    摘要

    In Western regions, tensions surrounding mediated nation-building progressively converge with growing commercialization of cultural institutions. The Story of Flanders (2023), a docudrama that narrates the genesis of Belgium’s northernmost region, proves exemplary of how reimaging national identity through public service media heightens fears for instrumentalization. This study investigates how heritage practitioners “make do” with the series and broader restrictions defined by austerity and political fragmentation. Despite ample research on historical television, identity and production, works commonly engage with historians as key expert contributors. Yet, important alternative roles have been neglected as central to the construction of televisual history. Results show austerity-drive imperatives guide practitioners to leverage discreet tactics to navigate this sensitive climate. Three areas of tension manifest, as the burden of history is weighted down by an added ideological layer: anticipating political fragmentation and fostering trust, negotiating authority and authorship, and facilitating dialogue and navigating austerity.

  8. Why does distant suffering matter? Discursive domestication in Chinese mainstream media and social media on the Shanghai Jewish Refugees Museum
    Yefu Qian, Xueying Wang · pp. 1662-1681 · online 2026-07-25
    摘要

    This study examines how distant suffering is domesticated across Chinese mainstream media and social media through the case of the Shanghai Jewish Refugees Museum. Existing research has largely focused on media representations and audience responses to mediated suffering events, paying less attention to commemorative spaces and users’ digital participation in non-Western contexts. Drawing on critical discourse analysis of mainstream media texts and Xiaohongshu posts, this study identifies three institutional domestication strategies, namely emotional, familiarizing and moral domestication, and shows how social media users re-domesticate commemorative meaning through affective resonance, geopolitical reframing and everyday spatial practices. The findings reveal that social media participation neither simply accepts nor rejects institutional narratives but produces a negotiated field of meaning shaped by platformed affect, contemporary geopolitical imagination and urban spatial consumption. By conceptualizing this process as participatory re-domestication, the article extends the study of distant suffering from media events to commemorative spaces, showing that spatial encounters with distant suffering do not automatically generate cosmopolitan empathy but are filtered through local platform practices and ideological structures.

  9. Contextualising the precarities of social media influencership in Nigeria
    Muhammed Tobiloba Alakitan · pp. 1682-1704 · online 2026-08-04
    摘要

    This study draws on and extends Duffy et al.’s framework of nested precarities of visibility by conceptualising platforms, brands and the Nigerian state power geometry through the situatedness of Nigerian X influencers’ digital labour and entrepreneurship experiences. I argue that platform algorithms, markets and ecology operate within contexts that disproportionately affect Nigerian influencers. First, algorithmic suspensions cause influencers to lose their accounts for opaque and discriminatory reasons, particularly along capitalist, cultural and political lines. Second, the X creator revenue-sharing programme does not reward influencers equally because of its geopolitical and discriminatory criteria for earnings. Third, the exploitative, bureaucratic and gendered nature of the Nigerian influencer entrepreneurship industry drives up the cost of campaigns and forces micro-influencers to do more for less. Fourth, the state’s restrictive policies, such as the 2021 Twitter ban, and ambiguous taxation laws, including the 2025 Nigeria Tax Act, significantly affect influencers. These findings, engaging Africa’s scholarship on creative labour and the digital economy, contribute to de-westernising media and communication studies, as Nigerian influencers’ experiences serve as a normative critique of platforms, brands and the state’s hegemonic power. I rely on in-depth interviews of 15 influencers and representatives of corporate organisations and PR agencies.

  10. Mapping worker-led AI governance in the global cultural industries
    Rafael Grohmann, Helena Wright · pp. 1705-1718 · online 2026-07-21
    摘要

    Between 2022 and early 2026, hundreds of cultural workers’ organizations around the world have mobilized in response to generative AI. From the Hollywood strikes of 2023 to voice actors’ protests in Mexico City, from pan-European game workers’ declarations to the first workers’ appendix to Brazil’s national AI bill, these mobilizations are reshaping how AI is deployed across cultural industries. This article presents a tracker of worker-led AI governance, documenting 142 worker organizations in 34 countries across eight cultural sectors. The tracker’s evidence suggests that collective bargaining is one of eight repertoires documented in the field, and not the most frequent; that most organizations target the state rather than employers or AI companies; that workers’ claims cluster around compensation, consent and copyright, with unauthorized training on creative output as the most cited harm; and that voice acting, the sector with most presence of Majority World, mobilizes a cultural-identity register that tracks the sector’s position in global screen value chains.

  11. Connectivity justice: A new concept for the post-digital society
    André Jansson, Cornelia Brantner, Karin Fast, Linda Ryan Bengtsson · pp. 1719-1732 · online 2026-07-23
    摘要

    This theoretical commentary introduces the concept of connectivity justice and discusses how it can be utilized in research on mobile, digitally reliant lives. Connectivity justice reframes questions of mobility and data justice for a post-digital world in which digital connections have become both indispensable and intrusive. The concept extends recent work on digital disconnection by examining the institutional and sociotechnical drivers of connectivity (in)justice, such as state actors, tech industry, labor conditions, and cultural norms, rather than individual-level correlates alone. By combining the capabilities approach with a feminist ethics of care, connectivity justice highlights that justice today involves not only equitable access to infrastructures but also the relational capacity to connect well, to engage, withdraw, and sustain digital life responsibly. This lens opens new directions for mobilities research, and beyond, inviting analyses of how digital infrastructures, labor platforms, and everyday practices shape people’s freedoms to move, communicate, and care. In doing so, it bridges normative and empirical inquiry, providing a vocabulary for understanding justice as an evolving balance between autonomy and interdependence within networked societies.

  12. The nation’s third body: Shein’s paradox between performed nationhood and transnational operations
    Liming Liu · pp. 1733-1745 · online 2026-07-23
    摘要

    The intensifying global tariff tensions are reshaping the operations of cross-border e-commerce platforms. This study examines Shein, a Chinese-origin e-commerce platform with global reach, to explore nationhood and transnational operations amid emerging tensions through a communication lens. Conducting Shein as a case study, this paper draws on the nation’s two bodies to conceptualize the third body as a distinct form of nationhood in the platform age. Findings reveal that Shein enacted a deterritorialized yet embedded nationhood, performing through logistical sovereignty, digital infrastructure, and transnational governance. These dimensions indicate that the third body provides insight into how state power can remain invisible yet operate through platforms. This paper concludes with implications for future research on the infrastructural turn of platforms in today’s global ambivalent moment.

  2. Britain’s State-Endorsed ‘Radical Left’: Fireweed , A Magazine of Working-Class and Socialist Arts, 1975–1978
    Nick Lee · pp. 6-30 · online 2025-12-08
    摘要

    This article presents the first history of Fireweed, ‘a magazine of working-class and socialist arts’ and in so doing reveals a forgotten contribution to British literary and artistic culture. This article argues that Fireweed—overlooked by existing research—is worthy of rediscovery owing to its improbable Arts Council funding and its unique blend of elements: it was multi-disciplinary, mixed-media and featured the work of amateur and professional contributors. Fireweed is remarkable because of its emergence from a Lancashire writers’ group, its avowed socialist leaning and its endorsement by the British state; its pages offer a vivid insight into the cultural and political life of the 1970s.

  3. Selective Racism, the Daily Mail , and the Anglo-Japanese Alliance
    Shitong Ding · pp. 31-45 · online 2026-03-03
    摘要

    This article examines the role of the popular press, particularly the Daily Mail, in shaping public opinion to facilitate the Anglo-Japanese Alliance of 1902. It focuses on the role and writing of Herbert Wrigley Wilson, long-time contributor and assistant editor of the Mail. It is argued that, especially through Wilson’s writings, the Daily Mail sought to recast Japan’s image in British public opinion from negative to positive, and to create an atmosphere more conducive to alliance. The ‘selective racism’ of the Mail is shown—defending Japan in racial terms while publishing intensely racist statements regarding South African Boers and Eastern European Jewish immigrants. This was not driven by racial hierarchy but by support for the realist imperatives of the British Empire—subordinating racial prejudice to strategic needs. This study presents a significant correction to scholarship on the alliance focused solely upon elite diplomacy and discourse.

  4. Capturing the Audience: Advertising’s Role in American Journalism in Historical and Critical Perspective
    Matthew L. Conaty, Victor Pickard · pp. 46-62 · online 2026-03-16

By historicizing the complicated relationship between journalism and advertising and arraying its critical historiography, we arrive at implications vital to the industry’s foundations and future.

  5. Representations of the Peace of MÜNSTER: News Genres and Communication Networks in Early Modern Spain (1647–1648)
    Eduardo Comerlato · pp. 63-79 · online 2026-04-20
    摘要

    This article examines Spanish representations in printed news pamphlets of the Peace of Münster, a treaty that recognised the independence of the United Provinces of the Netherlands from Spain and concluded the Eighty Years’ War. Often considered part of the broader Peace of Westphalia, the agreement was made possible after years of diplomatic negotiations in Münster. This study analyses two news pamphlets printed in Spain reporting on the agreement signed on 8 January 1647 and the treaty’s ratification on 15 May 1648. It explores news genres, communication networks, discourse and representations, offering insights into the early modern communicative landscape and how power can be exercised through texts.

  1. ‘Their problems are in many ways ours too’ – The Tanzanian Ujamaa -philosophy in Swedish foreign aid information and the Karibu Campaign of 1974
    Lars Diurlin · pp. 1-19 · online 2026-10-19
    摘要

    This article examines Karibu, a 1974 nation-wide Swedish governmental information campaign about Tanzania aimed at influencing public opinion in favor of Sweden's aid policies. The campaign praised President Julius Nyerere's Ujamaa-philosophy of communal farming even as it turned into coercive displacement of millions of Tanzanians. Drawing from archival sources and read against research on Swedish foreign aid history and issues of representation in development aid discourses, the study is driven by the assumption that post-colonial aid-relations share with colonialism the perceived necessity for the donor/colonizer to justify its actions by communicating ‘knowledge’ about the aid-receiver/colonized subject back to the home-front. The result shows that such representations do not necessarily uphold the traditional interpretative privilege and agency of the Global North. In Karibu, the Ujamaa-philosophy saturated the campaign to such an extent that it even shaped Swedish agency in blind compliance with Nyerere's development politics.

  2. From the Digital City to the Network Society: Tracing Sociotechnical Imaginaries of the Dutch Web (1994-2004)
    Nathalie Fridzema, Susan Aasman, Tom Slootweg, Rik Smit · pp. 1-21 · online 2026-09-24
    摘要

    This article examines how the meanings of ‘the web’ were constructed and contested in the Netherlands between 1994 and 2004. Drawing on a wide range of public sources, the study identifies four overlapping sociotechnical imaginaries. Each framed the web differently, respectively as civic infrastructure, a state-led modernisation project, speculative engine, and everyday network, and each drew on distinct social groups with unequal reach and institutional authority. Treating public discourse as the primary arena in which the web’s meaning, morality, and materiality were negotiated, the article contributes to the historiography of the early Dutch web and to broader debates in media history about how newly emerging technologies acquire social meaning. The early web in the Netherlands offers a valuable case as an infrastructural frontrunner, positioned at the intersection of American techno-entrepreneurial imaginaries and European policy ambitions, yet largely absent from existing web historiography.

  3. The 1897 Visit of King Chulalongkorn to Italy: A Media Frame Analysis of Italian Newspaper Articles
    Fabrizio Martino · pp. 1-21 · online 2026-09-21
    摘要

    This study examines how the Italian press represented the King of Siam, Chulalongkorn, during his first European tour in 1897, which marked a fundamental step in the rapprochement between the West and the East.   4. Tracing the Turkish modernization: A content analysis of newspaper ads depicting children in the Early Republican Period (1923–1950)
    Uğur Bakır, Murat Çelik, Süleyman Aşık · pp. 1-28 · online 2026-09-16
    摘要

    Children were positioned as one of the essential symbols in the dissemination of Turkish modernization discourse. This study examines the portrayal of children in advertisements during the early years of the Turkish Republic, aiming to explore how the ‘modern’ Turkish child was conceptualized in mass media. Through a content analysis of 491 advertisements published in national newspapers, the study reveals that children’s representation in advertisements played a significant role in negotiating broader processes of social and cultural transformation.   5. ‘Covering Our Tracks’: Archival Research on Liberation Movement Communication in South Africa, 1963–1980 | Limits and Possibilities
    Martha Evans · pp. 1-19 · online 2026-09-07
    摘要

    Archival research in post-colonial contexts poses challenges to researchers, with archives often telling us more about their creators than their subjects. In the modern South African context, a thirty-year period of censorship led to further silencings in the media and greatly affected the ways in which the liberation movement communicated. In order to avoid capture, activists rejected the practice of collating their communicative processes, covering their tracks, as it were, and leaving distinctly thin, sometimes coded, paper trails. This paper addresses the domino effect of censorship on liberation movement communication practices between 1963 and 1980, examining the ways in which state suppression forced resistance underground, posing various challenges (and opportunities) for historians. The paper argues that decolonial approaches to research—foregrounding resistance, orality and moving beyond conventional understandings of the archive—are best suited to researching dissident communication.

  6. Eclipse in the Press. The Circulation of News on the 1919 Total Solar Eclipse
    Ana Simões, Luís Miguel Carolino, Samuel Gessner, Hugo Soares, Cristina Luis · pp. 1-20 · online 2026-06-21
    摘要

    On 29 May 1919, one of the most famous total solar eclipses in history provided a crucial test for Albert Einstein’s theory of general relativity. During approximately five minutes of totality, British astronomers photographed the star field behind the eclipsed Sun to try to detect the predicted bending of light. Their successful confirmation transformed Einstein into an international icon, and newspapers worldwide reported the results. This article examines how news of the eclipse and the scientific expeditions spread through the global press. Rather than focussing on the later impact of Einstein’s relativity, it examines coverage of the expeditions themselves. Digital newspaper archives from Portugal, Brazil, Great Britain, Germany and the United States of America were surveyed for all eclipse-related news published between 1 January 1919 and 31 December 1920. This analysis provides the first systematic overview of how information about the eclipse and the expeditions circulated internationally, reflecting its transmission across different audiences in Europe and the Americas.

  7. Reading, writing, rumour: press readership and the making of war knowledge in Australia 1914–1918
    Bart Ziino · pp. 1-15 · online 2026-05-28
    摘要

    Studies of the media during the First World War have highlighted its weaknesses and deficiencies, yet the question of how citizens actually engaged with the press and made sense of the war remains elusive. Based on extensive analysis of Australian civilians’ correspondence, this article argues that citizens were capable of engaged and effective reading of the forms of information available in the press, including reportage, casualty lists and extracts from soldiers’ letters. That sensitive reading of the press, made in conjunction with their own contacts with soldiers on and returned from the front, allowed civilians to achieve a genuine, if necessarily imperfect, understanding of the nature, course and shocking costs of the war. Far from being entirely deceived, Australians’ determination to continue the struggle suggests they fought with their eyes open, even as they longed for the conflict to end.

  8. Propaganda in Pre-Soviet Caucasian Press at The Example of Comparative Analysis of Georgian Newspapers: ‘Ertoba’ (1919) and ‘Sakartvelo’ (1919)
    Tsitsino Bukia · pp. 1-10 · online 2026-05-15
    摘要

    In Georgia, as well as in Transcaucasia in general, from the first appearance of print media until the beginning of the twentieth century, only the interests of Tsarism were pursued in the press. The Tsarist and Communist press used similar propaganda styles while advancing different political interests. The shifting political climate of the transition period fostered media content distinct from Tsarist influence and shaped by relative freedom. Propaganda texts were, of course, still to be found in the pre-Soviet Caucasian press, but pluralism, the striving for independence of the countries conquered by the Russian Empire, and chaos created a different situation. This paper discusses the peculiarities of the propaganda activities of the pre-Soviet Caucasian press of the beginning of the twentieth century using the Georgian newspapers published in 1919: the social-democratic, Ertoba (Unity) and the national-democratic, Sakartvelo (Georgia).

  9. Transoceanic News for the Overseas Possessions: The Distribution of Periodical Publications ( Impresos ) in Spanish America, 1764–1781
    Nelson Fernando González Martínez · pp. 1-13 · online 2026-04-21
    摘要

    This essay examines the dynamics that facilitated the circulation of periodical publications (impresos) produced in Spain across the Spanish Atlantic during the second half of the eighteenth century. The category of impresos includes three periodicals published in Madrid: theGaceta de Madrid (Madrid Gazette), the Mercurio Histórico Político (Historical-Political Mercury), and the Guía de Forasteros (Foreigners’ Guide). These impresos spread news and reflections about Europe, Africa, and Asia. It is argued that the postal administrations created by the Spanish Empire after 1764 were fundamental to the circulation of printed news to Spanish America. The urban post offices established by the Bourbon government not only facilitated distribution but also served as centers for sales. Similarly, price reductions, tax exemptions, and frequent distribution encouraged the purchase of these materials. This study contributes to an understanding of how inhabitants of Spanish American territories accessed news from other parts of the world and the mechanisms that made this possible.

  10. Archival Marginalia: Metadata as Discursive Practice and Epistemic Redress in Diasporic Audiovisual Heritages
    Asli Özgen · pp. 1-25 · online 2026-04-20
    摘要

    Researching the film and media histories of migrant and diasporic communities presents significant archival challenges due to both the complex nature of audiovisual media and the historical neglect by memory institutions. While critical archival scholarship has emphasized proactive acquisition and appraisal to counter archival absences, this article argues that equal attention must be paid to the role of metadata practices. Building the concept of “archival marginalia,” the article proposes a framework for understanding archival metadata as a site of epistemic redress, drawing from decolonial and reparative archival theories.   11. The Regency Crisis and the Development of Organised Adversarial Party-Political Press in Britain
    James E. Peate · pp. 1-13 · online 2026-04-16
    摘要

    With the incapacity of King George III in 1788 and early 1789, a political crisis developed as the opposition Whigs looked to force a regency for the Prince of Wales, which would potentially see the fall of the Pittite government. The political machinations of this unsuccessful parliamentary campaign are well established; however, the political battle was not only fought in parliament but at length through the press. This article examines the press war that was fought between the opposition and the government. It shows that the developments in opposition party political management in the 1780s meant that the press war during the Regency Crisis represented a further development in the organisation of adversarial party politics. This was established by parliamentary and press arguments which were intrinsically intertwined for both opposition and government across newspapers, pamphlets and graphic satire as well as in Westminster.

  12. Airborne Coloniality Aerial Media and the Vertical Politics of Mediation and Violence
    Burçe Çelik, Sebastian James Rose · pp. 1-19 · online 2026-04-01
    摘要

    This article explores the colonial foundations of aerial media—those that operate in and as the atmosphere, from drones and satellites to data signals. Rather than simply transmitting through air, these media territorialise it, transforming the sky into a site of surveillance, control, and communicative violence. While some media scholarship has treated air as an elemental medium for sensory connection, its colonial-military histories remain largely overlooked. Drawing on archival research and engaging the anticolonial turn in media studies, this article examines how air was organised as a vertical medium of domination in 1920s Iraq under British mandate rule. Through wireless telegraphy, aerial photography, and bombardment, empire restructured the atmosphere into a communicative field of power. This study reframes air not as a neutral conduit of meaning, but as a historically saturated medium through which empire sees, knows, and breathes force—remaking how life is sensed, valued, governed and amputated from above.

  13. The risks of taking journalists for a ride during the Anglo-Irish War
    Michael Greasley · pp. 1-12 · online 2026-03-10
    摘要

    This article focuses on the aftermath of a skirmish between British forces and the Irish Republican Army [IRA] in November 1920 in a remote village near the town of Tralee in the south-west of Ireland. It examines newspaper reports of the incident and an accompanying photograph that purported to show three dead ambushers. The photograph was, in fact, staged after the event in a different location to provide some positive news during a difficult period for the British administration in Ireland. The discovery of the fraud undermined British efforts to cultivate positive relations with the national and international media. The fraudulent nature of the photograph has long been known to Irish historians, but this article examines its appearance in the context of the broader reporting of the crisis by English daily national and provincial newspapers. Finally, this piece also uses the skirmish to examine the creation of stock film footage of the Irish conflict by Pathé News that could be re-produced as news reporting in different contexts.

  1. AANZCA2025: ‘Turning the tides’: communication studies in an age of epistemic flux
    Harry Dugmore, Susan Grantham, Phoebe Macrossan · pp. 3-14 · online 2026-10-07
    摘要

    This introduction situates the nine papers selected for this Media International Australia AANZCA2025 Feature Topic. These papers were originally presented at the 2025 Australian and Aotearoa New Zealand Communication Association (AANZCA) conference hosted by the University of the Sunshine Coast (26–28 November 2025) amid an accelerating crisis of credibility in knowledge production. Social media and generative AI have deepened the destabilisation of various knowledge orders and fields of expertise, i.e., who gets to make knowledge and judge its veracity. This has reshaped public trust in expertise in general and journalistic knowledge production in particular, with profound implications for civic engagement and public deliberation. We argue that communication and media studies must continue recalibrating its theoretical and methodological tools to help turn the ‘tides’ of disengagement and democratic backsliding. Collectively, the nine papers in this Feature Topic demonstrate that the ongoing epistemic flux in our society and in our scholarship is not a uniform phenomenon, but one that plays out unevenly across audiences, industries and platforms, producing both new vulnerabilities and new forms of resistance and resilience.

  2. Mis- and distrust cues: investigating how people determine who and what to trust online
    Tanya Notley, Aimee Hourigan, Sora Park, TJ Thomson · pp. 15-32 · online 2026-07-06
    摘要

    The sheer volume of news and information people see online, especially on social media, can be overwhelming and make it difficult – or impossible – for people to fact-check the content they come across on a daily basis. In light of this, our study explored the everyday methods people use to decide who and what to trust online. Using a 1-week digital diary study with 55 adult participants living in Australia, we learnt that mis- and distrust cues provide people with a coping mechanism to deal with information overload. However, when we fact-checked content participants labelled as untrustworthy, 39% of these examples were factually correct. This suggests that people's hypervigilance in using mis- and distrust cues results in them frequently mis-categorising content. We draw on these findings to make recommendations relevant to news publishers and media literacy practitioners.

  3. Searching for the truth? search engines and their AI affordances’ responses to conspiratorial search practices
    Kateryna Kasianenko, Caroline Gardam, Katherine M. FitzGerald, Ashwin Nagappa, Axel Bruns, Shir Weinbrand et al. · pp. 33-55 · online 2026-09-01
    摘要

    Search engines are a common pathway to conspiracy theories, and producers of conspiratorial narratives actively encourage users to search for particular terms. Understanding how search engines respond to different information-seeking practices is therefore essential for explaining the dynamics of online conspiracy theories. Existing audits of search engines rarely account for systematic variation in query formulation arising from users’ differing levels of engagement with conspiratorial beliefs, nor do they consider recently introduced AI-powered search features. To address this gap, we developed search query sets informed by conspiratorial and non-conspiratorial information-seeking practices of online forums, using the chemtrails and 15-minute cities conspiracy theories as case studies. We performed algorithmic audits of Google's first-page search results and AI Overview responses. Our findings show substantial differences in the information returned for queries representative of general public information seeking compared with those reflecting conspiratorial perspectives. These differences appear to arise partly from Google's efforts to moderate sensitive topics, but also from the limitations of those interventions in recognising and responding to conspiratorial modes of query formulation. AI Overviews likewise vary according to query framing and generally attempt to debunk conspiratorial claims. However, these responses are often brief, provide limited supporting evidence, and are less effective for the newer 15-minute cities conspiracy theory, suggesting that current AI-mediated search guardrails remain uneven and require further development.

  4. Did journalists ‘sanewash’ Donald Trump during the 2024 election campaign? an empirical analysis
    Stephen Harrington, Timothy Graham, Ella Chorazy, Kristy Hess · pp. 56-79 · online 2026-08-11
    摘要

    Donald Trump's political and rhetorical style has presented a challenge to journalists’ modus operandi since he first announced his candidacy for President. During the 2024 election campaign, the practice of ‘sanewashing’ became a talking point among media and political commentators. The term describes the way Donald Trump's rambling, generally unfocussed speech would appear more coherent (and ‘sane’) when it was presented in news reports. In this paper, we empirically test whether ‘sanewashing’ occurred during the 2024 election campaign, focussing on two speeches that drew the most criticism for how they were covered in the media: one delivered to the Economic Club of New York, and the other given at a rally in Latrobe, Pennsylvania. Using topic modelling and semantic similarity analysis, we examine which components were covered and the language used in reporting. We conclude that while ‘sanewashing’ did occur, this may reflect standard journalistic procedures, rather than ideological motivations.

  5. From national outlets to transnational audiences: far-right hyperpartisan news sources on YouTube
    Klaus Groebner · pp. 80-98 · online 2026-08-11
    摘要

    In the past two decades, far-right movements have gained significant power in a variety of countries around the world, coinciding with increasing cooperation between far-right actors across borders. While the transnational convergence of these movements has been documented at elite and institutional levels, there has been limited investigation into the transnationalisation of the digital environments through which far-right narratives circulate. This article addresses this gap by examining the transnational far right through the lens of hyperpartisan news sources on YouTube. Through a novel data-gathering method, this study constructs a unique dataset comprising metadata from 114,025 videos and 51,449,494 comments across the entire lifetime of four channels, each based in a different English-speaking country. Using a unique combination of digital methods, including social network analysis and topic modelling, this article documents the content strategies of these hyperpartisan news sources and the resulting audiences across the entire lifespan of the channels. The findings reveal a highly integrated transnational far-right information space on YouTube with significant and sustained audience overlaps between the channels. Furthermore, the analysis identifies several strategies used by hyperpartisan news sources to attract and engage international audiences and raises critical questions about the transnational influence of the far right from the United States.

    Himani Joshi · pp. 99-115 · online 2026-09-08
    摘要

    When grief becomes social media content, boundaries between private mourning and public spectacle dissolve.   7. The cosy-turn: theorising cosy community as emergent counter-infrastructure in platform capitalism
    Venessa Paech, Jennifer Beckett · pp. 116-131 · online 2026-08-01
    摘要

    As trust in centralised social media platforms declines amid algorithmic harm, extractive data economies, and the erosion of psychological and epistemic safety, users are migrating toward smaller, semi-private digital spaces – a phenomenon some design and technology practitioners have termed the “cozy web.” While this shift is commonly characterised as retreat from digital publics, this paper offers the first sustained academic theorisation of the cosy web and the communities it gives rise to as a re-decentralisation of online social life and governance. Drawing on media and communication scholarship, feminist care ethics, and infrastructural critique, we conceptualise cosy communities as a distinct sociotechnical formation organised around care-centred governance, deliberate boundary-setting, and relational accountability – rather than visibility, automation, and scale. We build on the concept of cosy agency to describe the active work through which participants reconstruct stable, bounded digital environments in response to platform volatility, and draw on Giddens’ ontological security and Kelty's recursive publics to theorise the conditions under which such communities emerge and govern themselves. Examining three key terrains – decentralised and federated environments, independent hosted platforms, and enclaves within Big Social – we argue that cosy communities are not merely nostalgic retreats, but emergent counter-infrastructures that relocate governance from opaque platform systems to situated human judgement. The ‘cosy-turn’, we contend, represents a politics of maintenance and a reassertion of community as a governable social form within and against platform capitalism.

  8. AI-mediated witnessing: refugee testimony and visibility in Australia's Exhibit A-I
    Bridget Tombleson, Katharina Wolf · pp. 132-149 · online 2026-09-11
    摘要

    This article examines Exhibit A-I: The Refugee Account as a case of AI-mediated witnessing, exploring how ethical storytelling practices can centre marginalised voices without exposing vulnerable communities to further harm. Situated within Australia's offshore detention regime, which is characterised by restricted access and limited visual documentation, Exhibit A-I uses AI-generated imagery and synthetic audio to translate refugee testimony into publicly circulating artefacts.   9. The limits of disruption: AI and the persistence of VFX labour
    AD Narayan, Angelique Nairn, Justin Matthews, Duncan Caillard · pp. 150-171 · online 2026-07-31
    摘要

    Artificial intelligence (AI) and generative AI are increasingly framed as transformative forces in film production, particularly visual effects (VFX).   10. Human versus AI-generated emergency alerts: a comparative study for AI emergency communication applications
    Manolo Iachizzi, Susan Grantham · pp. 172-190 · online 2026-09-21
    摘要

    This study explores the capabilities of artificial intelligence (AI) to generate emergency alerts, using Tropical Cyclone Kirrily as a case study, which made landfall in Queensland, Australia, on 25 January 2024. ChatGPT-4 was used to recreate official alerts issued during the event via structured prompts informed by the Internalisation, Distribution, Explanation, Action (IDEA) model and Australian disaster communication guidelines. Findings indicate that AI-generated alerts largely satisfied the core IDEA model components and demonstrated a strong ability to personalise messages for specific needs and audiences with clear safety instructions.   11. What factors influence Australian media professionals’ level of agreement with guidelines for reporting suicide?
    Jaelea Skehan, Ross Tynan, Renate Thienel, Jane Pirkis, Myfanwy Maple, Brian Kelly · pp. 191-206 · online 2025-01-12
    摘要

    This study examined whether Australian media professionals agree with guidelines for reporting suicide (Mindframe guidelines) and the factors that may influence their level of agreement. Participants completed an online survey to assess their level of agreement with Mindframe guidelines and to explore factors that might be associated with guideline agreement. These factors included demographic variables, suicide stigma, exposure to suicide, perceived strength of the evidence, personal use of guidelines, organisational compliance with guidelines and prior exposure to Mindframe.   12. The “renaissance” of auditory medium in China: a study on the motives of Chinese podcasting users and corresponding development strategies of Chinese podcasting
    Ying Zhu, Yali Yang · pp. 207-235 · online 2025-01-07
    摘要

    In 2020, China witnessed its own “Year of the Podcast.” Podcast, an auditory medium that dominates the Western market, is once again enjoying a “renaissance” in China after years of silence. This article begins by tracing the development trajectory of Chinese podcasting and examining the reasons behind its “renaissance.” Then, based on Grounded Theory Methodology, this article utilizes user reviews from Little Universe, the first Chinese RSS podcast application on the App Store, as research samples. Assisted by qualitative research software Nvivo12, it analyzes the motives of Chinese podcasting users and the corresponding development strategies of Chinese podcasting. Unlike Western podcasting, social needs play a crucial role in Chinese podcasting, but there are divergent opinions among Chinese users regarding them. Therefore, the development of social functions needs to be carefully controlled within certain limits.

    Caitlin Parker · pp. 265-278 · online 2025-04-30
    摘要

Vol. 20, No. 1 · 2026-07-02 · 6 篇 · Liverpool UP · Film/Sound

  2. Foregrounding Hiss: Debord, Adorno, and Akerman in Sonata Form
    Sergio de Iudicibus · pp. 3-23
    摘要

    Despite its presence as both an embedded element in film optical tracks and as sound in cinema loudspeakers, hiss – present in all films from the earliest talkies to Dolby-encoded titles – has been largely ignored by media scholars. The present study ruptures this silence by acknowledging the sound of hiss as a meaningful component of the cinematic apparatus and experience. To do so, this essay – structured in sonata form – contextualises and juxtaposes Debord’s Howls for Sade, Adorno’s scholarship on radio, and Chantal Akerman’s News for Home, all of which foreground hiss as they engage with or disrupt the notion of ‘spectacle’. An examination of these sources and their historical constellations (elucidated through discussions on CBS, Dolby, and the Vitaphone as well as projection manuals) puts forth three theses: one, that the ‘visibility’ of hiss in cinema undergoes a century-long evolution, first as an engineering concern, later as an artistic device, and finally as a curiosity for digital spectators; two, that historical movements of attenuating or foregrounding hiss, often conceptualised as a visual phenomenon, trace political efforts of concealing and uncovering the cinematic apparatus, specifically the mediations responsible for the illusion of synchronisation; and three, that hiss in cinema ensures a pleasurable spectacular reassurance of formal continuity through sound.

  3. Inverting the Acousmêtre
    Jacques Dupuis · pp. 25-50
    摘要

    This article advances a revised conception of Michel Chion’s archetype of the acousmêtre in light of recent scholarship on narrative film and television. It begins by evaluating Chion’s criteria for the acousmêtre, arguing that its attendant traits are either essential or ancillary, contrary to Chion’s general implied necessity in traits he theorises. The article undertakes a set of inversions of ancillary traits to explore limitations and new applications of the trope as an interpretive lens. Through four primary case studies – Moon Knight (Jeremy Slater, 2022), Love Actually (Richard Curtis, 2003), Halloween (John Carpenter, 1978), and Parasite (Bong Joon Ho, 2019) – this expansion addresses a wider range of film and television genres than is typical for applications of the acousmêtre, including romantic comedy and family animation. These permutations bring out the acousmêtre’s ability to highlight structural power dynamics that might otherwise be overlooked, extending the trope’s relevance beyond characterisation and insular narrative concerns to implications of social commentary, an application of the trope that scholars seldom consider. The culminating analysis centres on the acousmêtre’s affordance of socio-economic critique in Bong’s Parasite.

  4. The Spectre of Film Music: A Hauntological Study of the Vaporwave Aesthetic in The Neon Demon (2016)
    Will Jeffery · pp. 51-73
    摘要

    This article explores the vaporwave aesthetic of The Neon Demon (Nicholas Winding Refn, 2016). Vaporwave is an electronic musical genre that emerged in the early 2010s, reconfiguring electronic music from the 1980s and 1990s. French philosopher Jacques Derrida coined the term ‘hauntology’ in the 1990s to describe a ‘thing’ from the past haunting the present, like a spectre that cannot be seen, through time being out of joint. Vaporwave music haunts The Neon Demon like Derrida’s spectre by harmonising audiovisually with the visual language of the film. I call this audiovisual harmony and haunting the vaporwave aesthetic. Set in Los Angeles in the present day, The Neon Demon follows Jesse, a 16-year-old orphan who moves to the city aspiring to become a fashion model. Jesse is lured by the vaporwave aesthetic’s futuristic, utopic guise which haunts the LA fashion industry, in the hope of achieving immortality in the industry after the decay of her traumatic past. However, Jesse is exposed, consumed, and ultimately killed by the spectral haunt of the fashion industry’s decayed capitalist utopia. The vaporwave aesthetic in The Neon Demon shows film music’s power to manipulate the film image.

  1. Affect and prediction in short-video social media recommendation algorithm: TikTok and the missing half-second
    Julia Salles · pp. 5093-5111 · online 2025-10-29
    摘要

  2. ChatGPT for cultural heritage and the customization of generative AI: A talkthrough analysis of the Luigi Einaudi chatbot
    Simone Natale, Bruno Surace, Enrico Mensa, Luca Befera · pp. 5112-5137 · online 2025-10-29
    摘要

    Cultural heritage institutions have recently experimented with the customization of generative AI and specifically large language models (LLMs) to create chatbots that impersonate historical characters.   3. Warm experts among us: Conceptualising the challenges of informal digital support for older adults
    Riitta Hänninen, Sakari Taipale · pp. 5138-5156 · online 2025-10-29
    摘要

    This critical conceptual analysis explores the key challenges associated with warm experts, such as family members and friends, in providing informal digital support in later life. We examine the challenges that stem from the dyadic relationship between warm experts and older adults, as well as the individual, social and societal characteristics of being a warm expert with limited resources to provide digital support to older adults. In conclusion, while the contribution of warm experts to digital support in later life is evident, they may lack the necessary resources to meet the heterogeneous needs of older adults from an individual standpoint.   4. “On TikTok, everything needs to be playful, even the Holocaust!”: Playability, memes, and participatory memory culture
    Tom Divon, Tobias Ebbrecht-Hartmann · pp. 5157-5188 · online 2025-10-29
    摘要

    TikTok has become a space where playfulness is the dominant lingo for engaging with serious subjects. On the platform, the Holocaust is mediated through meme-based performances like the #POVHolocaustChallenge, a trend that invited users to re-enact fictionalized memories of Holocaust victims. Our analysis of 250 videos identifies 3 types of Holocaust-related memetic narratives—mem(e)ories: (1) Testimonial Mem(e)ory, where “victims” testify from “heaven” as confessional storytelling; (2) Punitive Mem(e)ory, where “victims” choose to be sent back to the Holocaust as punishment; and (3) Escapist Mem(e)ory, where “victims” time travel from the 1940s to the present for a brief escape. Drawing on interviews with 15 creators and 7 Holocaust institution representatives, we argue that #POV challenges re-mediate past events, transform into education, and serve as a means for forging personal connections to the Holocaust. We introduce TikTok’s affordance of playability as a structural condition that governs participation, with users merging performative storytelling with remix culture and inscribing Holocaust (post)memory into their platformed experiences.

  5. Infrastructural intermediation: Refining datafication, reinforcing commodification, and replenishing selection in the platformization of cultural production
    Zhongxuan Lin, Yinan Wang, Fangru Liu · pp. 5189-5208 · online 2025-11-02
    摘要

    This study analyzes how short-video platforms, with Douyin as a case, depend on cultural intermediaries (e.g. multi-channel networks and digital marketing agencies) to sustain operations and expand platform power. Unlike existing studies that exaggerate or underestimate cultural intermediaries’ agency, this study offers a middle-ground perspective and conceptualizes it as “infrastructural intermediation,” in which cultural intermediaries act as external dispositifs of platform power in cultural production. Using participant observation and interviews with intermediaries and creators, we identify three primary infrastructural functions: (1) visualizing: converting data into actionable guidance for platform strategy implementation; (2) reinforcing: optimizing creators’ practices to advance monetization; and (3) repairing: remedying deficiencies in technical and institutional mechanisms. As an indispensable component of the platform, they reinforce platformization structures, complete the “last mile” of platform power implementation. This study also reveals how China’s distinctive state-platform relationship shapes the role and strategies of cultural intermediaries.

  6. Study on identity construction of affinity group from the perspective of interaction ritual chains: A textual analysis of the “TikTok Refugee” discourse on REDNOTE
    Haonan Li, Yufan Liang · pp. 5209-5234 · online 2025-11-03

Using Collins’ Interaction Ritual Chains theory as a framework, we conducted participant observation, content analysis, and computational textual analysis of over 1000 high-engagement posts and 9000 comments under the #TikTokRefugee hashtag on REDNOTE.     摘要

    摘要

    We are now living in a platform society, where platforms are widely acknowledged to exert significant control over users. But how can user agency exert power to influence the platform?   9. The wellness pipeline: Tracing far-right health narratives on X
    Piotr Marczyński, Catherine Tebaldi · pp. 5291-5314 · online 2025-11-06
    摘要

    This article examines how far-right groups advance political agendas through digital platforms, specifically by embedding reactionary ideology within health and wellness discourse.   10. Digital-regional capital: Digitally enhanced bonds between Chinese internal migrants and their hometowns
    Yutian Xiong, Tingting Liu · pp. 5315-5334 · online 2025-11-14
    摘要

    This study proposes a Bourdieusian theory of digital-regional capital to explore the mediated bond between migrants and their hometowns, addressing an under-studied issue in the context of transregional mobility while filling a gap in the spatial dimension of prior digital capital studies. Focusing on the interplay among digital capital, internal migration, and the development of rural and inland regions in China, this research conducts interviews with 48 internal migrants and 2 government representatives, examining how China’s techno-nationalism/regionalism, combined with various digital platforms, particularly WeChat’s dominant role, has co-shaped digital-regional capital.   11. Gender and social media campaigning: Opportunities for women candidates to close the gender gap in Dutch politics?
    Maurice Vergeer · pp. 5335-5361 · online 2025-11-17
    摘要

    Gender inequality in Dutch politics remains persistent, with men dominating parliament. This study explores whether social media campaigning can help reduce this imbalance by examining how women and men differ in their online campaign strategies and how these differences affect likability on social media and electoral success. Drawing on the concept of trait-balancing, the study analyzes communication styles, issues, and sentiments in tweets from all parliamentary candidates using topic modeling. Results show that while men and women use similar communication styles, women benefit more from social and positive messaging, as well as participation in Q&A interactions—factors that predict higher preference votes.   12. Technolinguistic suspension and the digital futures of minority languages: AI and Meänkieli in Sweden
    Moa Eriksson Krutrök, Pär Poromaa Isling · pp. 5362-5389 · online 2025-11-14
    摘要

    As AI-driven language models transform communication, minority languages like Meänkieli—a national minority language in northern Sweden—face new forms of digital marginalization. Historically suppressed by the Swedish state, Meänkieli now risks exclusion from rapidly evolving AI ecosystems. This article examines how current AI platforms shape language inclusion, combining semi-structured interviews with language technologists, conservators, and promoters, and a platform analysis of AI translation and language tools. Drawing on these materials, we develop the concept of “technolinguistic suspension” to describe the state in which Meänkieli currently exists: symbolically recognized yet excluded from the technological infrastructures that increasingly define linguistic legitimacy. The study argues that overcoming technolinguistic suspension requires both structural reforms in platform design and sustained institutional commitment to minority language futures.

  13. Deplatforming Donald Trump influences platform migration between Twitter and alternative sites
    Longhan Wei, Tian Yang · pp. 5390-5413 · online 2025-11-17
    摘要

    Social media platforms have removed influential users for violating platform rules. While this intervention reduces harmful content on the platform, deplatforming high-profile figures may prompt general users to migrate to alternative domains, where they can continue to engage with inappropriate communication. Utilizing web tracking data from 12,400 US Twitter users, we showed that after Twitter deplatformed Donald Trump, users had no immediate change in Twitter usage, but their engagement with the platform decreased slightly over time. Meanwhile, they demonstrated an increase in visiting alternative domains, although this spike gradually tapered off. This pattern varied across the population: Those frequently consuming left-leaning news immediately increased Twitter usage, whereas users visiting right-leaning outlets displayed a rise in using alternative social media, especially among heavy consumers of right-leaning news. These findings highlight the importance of investigating two separate processes of platform migration, non-use and new use, for appraising the impact of content moderation.

  14. Enriching the metapicture: How to analyse audio-visual content on TikTok and other platforms
    Lucia Bainotti, Stijn Peeters · pp. 5414-5439 · online 2025-11-17
    摘要

    This article discusses a methodological approach to the study of audio-visual content on platforms like TikTok. We argue that existing methodologies for the analysis of visual content based on the notions of the metapicture are not yet fully equipped to account for the multi-layered, dynamic, and information-rich nature of audio-visual material. To fill this gap, we propose an approach aimed at ‘enriching’ the metapicture with textual and other components, alongside visual ones, to critically and systematically analyse video collections.   15. Everything to everyone, all at once: Digital human versions as objects of agency and surrender
    Leah Henrickson, Louise Santina Tompkins-Tinari · pp. 5440-5459 · online 2025-11-17
    摘要

    This article explores the sociocultural implications of digital human versions, drawing parallels between the self-objectification in Marina Abramović’s 1974 performance Rhythm 0 and that in recent AI-driven digital version CarynAI, a chatbot run by a social media influencer in 2023–2024. A digital version is a digital replica of an embodied human, living or dead, that convincingly mimics that person’s textual, visual and aural habits, and exists independently of that source person. By considering digital versions through the lens of (self-)objectification, this article argues that individuals simultaneously assert and relinquish power when creating their own digital versions. Ultimately, this article argues for the urgent need to understand the shifting dynamics of personal agency, interpersonal intimacy and identity posted by digital human versions.

  16. Politically influenced by nonpolitical actors? Longitudinal reciprocal associations between social media personalities’ political social media posts and political interest in adolescence
    Anaëlle J Gonzalez, Margot Lissens, Laura Vandenbosch · pp. 5460-5481 · online 2025-11-17
    摘要

    Social media personalities—including influencers, athletes, and celebrities who are typically nonpolitical figures, such as Taylor Swift—now serve as a primary news source for many adolescents on social media This study, grounded in Social Cognitive Theory, explores how exposure to political content from adolescents’ favorite influencers and celebrities on social media may reciprocally relate to their political interest. A three-wave panel study collected data among 520 French adolescents ( M age = 15.14, SD age = 1.90, 58.7% girls) in 2021–22 and was linked to content analytical data on the political posts of adolescents’ favorite accounts. Furthermore, neither peer nor parental political interest moderated these relationships. Explanations for the absence of media and selection effects are discussed.

  17. The rich social world of mobile and social media: Four pathways toward satisfying the need to belong
    Jeffrey A Hall, Liesel L Sharabi · pp. 5482-5501 · online 2025-11-17
    摘要

    This study outlines four pathways toward satisfying the need to belong through media use: relational remembrance, mediated social interaction, connection with content or target, and shared human experience. In study 1, participants ( N = 292) described moments of connection while using social media, and social interactions with existing relationship partners were most common. In study 2, participants ( N = 188) screenshotted moments ( n = 551) when they experienced social presence through media in daily life. Participants reported the degree to which they had an established relationship or a social interaction with the target, connected with the content or target absent any pre-existing relationship, and felt a shared experience. Multilevel models found all pathways were associated with social presence and perceived change in connection. Although social interactions within established relationships constitute most experiences of belongingness and social presence, a sense of identification, parasocial relationships, and online communities are other meaningful pathways.

  18. Ephemeral visibility and externally visible production: How algorithmic automation accelerates fans’ data work
    Anna Yan Liu · pp. 5502-5524 · online 2025-11-21
    摘要

    Algorithmic rankings like streaming charts automatically convert users’ behavioral traces into orders of content, shaping their public visibility and motivating fans’ data work to promote favored cultural products on rankings. Building on social acceleration and the sociology of quantification, we take a media sociology perspective to examine how algorithmic automation accelerates public visibility and fans’ data work. Based on netnography of a Chinese idol group INTO1’s fans, we theorize that algorithms, a technological acceleration in audience data production, trigger two changes in public visibility encountered by fans: ephemeral visibility and externally visible production.   19. Examining membership across multiple online communities in the “manosphere”
    Desislava Bocheva, Joanne Hinds, Olivia Brown, Lukas W. Mayer · pp. 5525-5549 · online 2025-11-20
    摘要

    Online communities are vast, complex spaces attracting diverse users, offering support and connection, but also spreading harmful content, including misogyny and extremism.   20. Exploring parental mediation trajectories and their associations with mental health and autonomy in adolescents: A latent transition analysis
    Sophie Mayen, Anne-Linda Camerini · pp. 5550-5570 · online 2025-11-20
    摘要

    Longitudinal research on the characteristics and dynamics of parental mediation strategies and their associations with adolescents’ mental health and autonomy is scarce. Using three-wave panel data from 717 Swiss adolescents (collected between spring 2018 and autumn 2020), we applied Latent Transition Analysis to identify distinct parental mediation classes and adolescents’ transitioning patterns, resulting in the: Enforcing & Engaged Approach; Hands-off Approach ; and Moderate Approach , showing fluctuation over time.     摘要

  22. The role of epistemic norms in mitigating the spread of misinformation
    Aviv Barnoy, Shirel Bakbani-Elkayam, Boaz Miller, Arnon Keren · pp. 5596-5619 · online 2025-11-20
    摘要

    This article highlights the role of epistemic norms in mitigating the spread of misinformation. The mixed-methods study includes exploratory reconstructions and survey experiments. Two intervention approaches proved efficient in reducing the sharing of misinformation, but only one significantly differentiated between true and false information. This study contributes to the literature on normative countermeasures and is the first to emphasize epistemic norms.   25. Scrolling social media with the third eye: Mindful technology use as a disconnection strategy
    Dicle Berfin Köse · pp. 5664-5683 · online 2025-11-24
    摘要

    Constant connectedness and social media overuse have prompted disconnection practices like quitting and digital detoxing. Besides these approaches, mindfulness stands as a promising quality to benefit from social media without being overwhelmed by their drawbacks. Accordingly, this study investigates whether mindful technology use (MTU) can help manage social media use. Based on the Affordance-Use-Reflection-Automaticity Model, MTU was proposed as a means to regulate the effects of social media affordances (i.e. hedonic and utilitarian content gratification), habit, satisfaction, and system use.   27. Unscheduled and unscripted: Uses, gratifications, and perceived affordances of the BeReal photo-sharing app
    Erin E Hollenbaugh, Emily C Keith, Katharine Wall · pp. 5709-5729 · online 2025-11-24
    摘要

    This study examined people’s motives, perceived affordances, and gratifications of using BeReal, a photo-sharing mobile social media app. Through an online survey of 146 English-speaking BeReal users, the study applied the affordance framework and uses and gratifications theory to analyze user experiences and perceptions. Findings revealed the most significant gratifications obtained were expressing the authentic self, entertainment, connecting to others, and presenting the ideal self, and these perceived affordances moderated the relationships between motives and gratifications obtained. Users seeking companionship were more likely to alleviate loneliness through BeReal when they perceived stronger social presence. Those using BeReal to pass time found it more entertaining when the app was seen as less editable and ephemeral. In addition, users aiming to connect with others experienced greater satisfaction when they perceived content as less editable. This study provides insights into how affordances impact how satisfied users are with BeReal’s ability to gratify their needs.

  31. Not the average user: A latent profile analysis of privacy protection appraisals in targeted advertising
    Laurent H Wang, Dina Naji Arch, Miriam J Metzger · pp. 5789-5810 · online 2025-12-01
    摘要

    Drawing from protection motivation theory (PMT), this study explored subgroups of Internet users based on their patterns of privacy threat and coping appraisals (i.e. perceived severity, perceived vulnerability, perceived benefits, self-efficacy, response efficacy, and response cost) in the context of targeted advertising. A latent profile analysis using a nationally-representative sample of US adults revealed three meaningful subgroups: Privacy-Optimistic, Privacy-Cautious, and Privacy-Balanced. These profiles differed in privacy protection intention, with Privacy-Cautious individuals reporting the highest intention, followed by Privacy-Balanced and Privacy-Optimistic individuals. In addition, higher perceived surveillance was associated with higher odds of being in the Privacy-Cautious and Privacy-Balanced (vs Privacy-Optimistic) profiles. Higher privacy powerlessness was also associated with higher odds of being in the Privacy-Cautious (vs Privacy-Optimistic) profile. Findings theoretically extend PMT and previous privacy research by highlighting the heterogeneity in users’ online privacy appraisals, and practically inform more tailored intervention and message designs.

  32. The impact of online harassment on trust: Survey evidence from Denmark and Norway
    Audun Fladmoe, Marjan Nadim · pp. 5811-5830 · online 2025-12-02
    摘要

    Online harassment is shown to have democratic consequences by limiting freedom of expression, disrupting public discourse, reinforcing prejudice and hostility, and creating barriers for online participation. However, there has been less attention to how online harassment relates to trust, which is a key component of well-functioning democracies. This study investigates the relationship between experiences with online harassment and different forms of social and institutional (media and governmental) trust. The analyses utilize survey panel data from Denmark and Norway, two high-trust societies. Findings indicate that online harassment, especially group-based harassment, negatively correlates with all forms of trust, with the most significant panel effects observed on governmental trust and trust in social media (Facebook), which are both institutions that regulate speech.   33. Ethical conundrums: Hacked data in the study of far-right violent extremism
    Lise Waldek, Brian Ballsun-Stanton, Muhammad Iqbal, David Kernot, Debra Smith · pp. 5831-5854 · online 2025-12-02
    摘要

    Ethical conduct in digital research is full of grey areas. Disciplinary, institutional and individual norms and conventions developed to support research are challenged, often leaving scholars with a sense of unease or lack of clarity. The growing availability of hacked data is one area. Discussions and debates around the use of these datasets in research are extremely limited. Reviews of the history, culture or morality of the act of hacking are topics that have attracted some scholarly attention. However, how to undertake research with this data is less examined and provides an opportunity for the generation of reflexive ethical practice. This article presents a case study outlining the ethical debates that arose when considering the use of hacked data to examine online far-right violent extremism. It argues that under certain circumstances, researchers can do ethical research with hacked data. However, to do so, we must proactively and continually engage deeply with ethical quandaries and dilemmas.
