The article ultimately shows how unity operates as both a strategy for legitimacy and a mechanism of labour discipline within global digital sexual economies.

  16. Eroticized evaluation: casting and labour incorporation in early postsocialist gay pornography
    Jonathan Dusenbury · pp. 1-16 · online 2026-07-29
    摘要

    This article examines Young Russian Innocents and Young Soldiers Disclosed, two 1994 transnational gay pornographic films featuring Russian performers. The article argues that their casting scenes constitute a distinct pornographic form in which recruitment, inspection, and conditional approval become the primary site of erotic investment. Performers are interviewed, translated, examined, undressed, and sexually tested on camera, transforming audition into a sustained structure of labour evaluation. The camera assumes an employer’s perspective, while the translator mediates linguistic and bodily legibility. Produced amid postsocialist economic instability, the films render market incorporation visible within the sexual encounter itself. Rather than treating pornography as merely representing precarious labour, the article shows how these films eroticize the evaluative procedures through which bodies become employable and incorporable within transnational markets.

  17. Naked indifferences: the ethics of (a)sexual representation
    Muskaan Katiyar · pp. 1-17 · online 2026-07-23
    摘要

    Where does the nude as a cyber-body-in-representation situate itself within the contemporary sexual oversaturation alongside the crisis of unfeeling and anxiety surrounding obscenity? Through an auto-theoretical approach, this article examines the circulation of my queer body as it negotiates with its ‘asexual resonances’. I locate my sexuality by looking at adolescence, with attention to the heteropatriarchal Indian discourse and the digital proliferation of pornography and sex as central to the establishment of ‘positive’ identity. I read this private visual modality alongside Shannon Bell’s conception of ‘pornosophy’, a concept that in my analysis allows for the reinstating of a critical distance between sexual representation and sexual acts. How does an alternate (a)sexual image expand this ethical distance and its relation to explicitness and obscurity? How do asexual aesthetics and sexual unfeeling recalibrate the sensational/unsensational dichotomy? I analyse this asexual impasse beyond the economy of sexual optimism and the historically negative construction of sexual coldness within the simultaneous emergence of sexology and popular pornography.

  18. Migrant men on OnlyFans: masculinity, precarity, and erotic entrepreneurship in the digital gig economy
    Yang Zhao, Garth Stahl, Rohann Irving · pp. 1-16 · online 2026-07-23
    摘要

    Recent research on OnlyFans creators has focused largely on women, leaving men’s participation comparatively underexamined. This conceptual article adopts an interdisciplinary lens to explore what migrant men’s engagement with OnlyFans may mean for them. We consider how the platform opens spaces for migrant men to commodify their bodies and generate income, often by mobilizing racialized and gendered stereotypes whereby such eroticized performances contribute to the reproduction of gender and sexuality norms. Empowerment framings of digitized sex work may risk obscuring the structural inequalities, emotional labour, and/or exploitation that shape platform labour. Rather than adopting empowerment versus exploitation dichotomies, we conceptualize OnlyFans as a site of contested autonomy for migrant men where their masculinities are refashioned through a mix of entrepreneurship, erotic self-branding, and the logics of platform capitalism. We call for deeper engagement with the gendered dimensions of migrancy – particularly migrant masculinities – in research on digitized sex work.

  19. Motivations and outcomes of women’s pornography use
    Katerina Litsou, Cynthia Graham, Nicholas Kelley, Roger Ingham · pp. 1-28 · online 2026-07-22
    摘要

    There is relatively limited information on why women use pornography, what they expect from it, and the emotions they experience after use. Conflicting findings have been reported about the association between pornography use and women’s sexual satisfaction. This study assessed motivations, outcomes, sexual satisfaction, and emotions associated with women’s pornography use. Women (N = 3330, mean age 27.5 years) reporting either solo or partnered pornography use at least once in the past six months completed an online survey. Single women reported more frequent pornography use than those in relationships. More frequent solo and partnered pornography use were both associated with higher sexual satisfaction. Women reported experiencing more positive emotions and fewer negative emotions after solo use. Partnered use was associated with lower levels of negative emotions and higher levels of positive emotions. A principal component analysis identified three main motivations: escape from boredom and entertainment; sexuality exploration and learning; and physical and psychological arousal. Another principal component analysis identified two outcome factors: negative and positive. These findings highlight the complex role of pornography in women’s sexual lives and may help to normalize pornography use and reduce stigma. Clinicians might suggest pornography as a tool to improve arousal, pleasure, and couple communication.

  20. Beyond harm narratives: reimagining porn education through ‘safe uncertainty’
    Emily Setty, Jonny Hunt · pp. 1-17 · online 2026-07-16
    摘要

    Contemporary UK policy and educational responses to pornography are largely organized around risk, harm, and prevention. Drawing on the UK Independent Pornography Review, this article examines how pornography is constructed as an object of governance and how this framing shapes pedagogy. While harm-prevention approaches prioritize certainty about meaning and effect, interdisciplinary research highlights young people’s interpretive diversity, ambivalence, and contextual negotiation. Pornography education is thus structured by a tension between institutional demands for clarity and the lived reality of uncertainty. To address this tension, the article develops ‘safe uncertainty’ as a pedagogical orientation that combines interpretive openness with relational and institutional safety. The article outlines how this approach can be operationalized in educational settings and considers its feasibility within current policy contexts. By reframing pornography education as engagement with uncertainty, the article contributes to debates on porn literacy, youth sexuality, and educational governance.

  2. Difficult Relations between the Rescuers and the Rescued: (In)Hospitality and (In)Gratitude in Contemporary Cinematic Superhero Genre
    Tomasz Jacheć · pp. 2-24
    摘要

    This article analyzes the emotional attitudes of hospitality, trust, and gratitude in the context of the rescuer(s)-rescued relations found in contemporary superhero films of the Marvel Cinematic Universe (MCU) and DC Universe (DCU). The analysis is based on Robert Jewett and John Shelton Lawrence's concept of the American monomyth. It would seem that the key factor for the monomythic adventures to even begin is the hospitality displayed by the threatened community toward the rescuer(s), and the trust laid upon them. And, at the end of a monomythic story, there ought to be expressions of gratitude on the part of the rescued toward their rescuer(s). However, the relations between the oppressed and their redeemers in the twenty-first-century monomythic stories are far more complex. In this article, I analyze factors and variables contributing to the complexity relations between the oppressed, the institutions representing the oppressed, and the (super)heroes.

  3. Quantitative Studies on Color and Film Genres: A Systematic Review
    Jaime López-Díez, Shu Hu, Lingfei Luan, Thomas Ackerman, Joseph P. Magliano · pp. 25-50
    摘要

    This study conducts a systematic review of quantitative research examining the relationship between color and film genres, specifically focusing on the attributes of hue, saturation, and brightness. An extensive search of Web of Science and Scopus identified eight important articles on US film productions published in English. Given that most prior studies in this domain have been qualitative, the advent of quantitative studies offers novel insights. The results suggest that the correlation between color and genre is predominantly modest; still, distinct patterns were seen, including low saturation in action and adventure films, high saturation in animated films, and low brightness in horror films. Proportional weights of the provided findings were computed to assess consistency across research, emphasizing limited still important genre-specific color traits.

  4. How Does Television Seriality Reshape Spectator Hypothesis Formation?: The Primacy of the Mesoscopic Level in Serial Engagement
    Roberto Balestri · pp. 51-70
    摘要

    Television seriality demands sustained cognitive engagement, as viewers form hypotheses to navigate complex narratives. This article argues that beyond moment-to-moment (micro) and ultimate outcome (macro) predictions, medium-term “mesoscopic hypotheses” dominate spectator activity. These concern the unfolding, intersection, and temporary resolution of plotlines and character arcs. We propose a three-level (micro–meso–macro) model of engagement, showing through series like Grey's Anatomy , Breaking Bad , and Game of Thrones how viewers focus on the “middle game.” Genres shape distinct hypothesis patterns, while fan speculation amplifies mesoscopic focus. This framework clarifies how serial narratives sustain pleasure and interest without definitive closure.

  5. Understanding Dementia: Puzzle Film Techniques and Affective Experience in The Father (2020)
    Rosemary Long · pp. 71-89
    摘要

    With a focus on cinematic subjectivity, this article examines how The Father (2020) uses its formal elements to create spatial, temporal, and narrative disruptions that replicate the effects of the cognitive deterioration experienced by people living with dementia. In doing so, it differs from most screen representations of mental disorders that show symptoms from the perspective of an observer rather than the person experiencing them, consequently objectifying them as “other.” By constructing a subjective portrayal of dementia, The Father —a puzzle film with a few pieces missing—crafts an immersive experience aligning the audience's perceptions with those of its protagonist, offering insight into the lived experience of dementia symptoms and thus the possibility of an affective, experiential understanding of this disease.

  6. Handheld in Enaction: First-Person Perspective of Professional Cinematographers
    Mehmet Burak Yilmaz, Willeke Rietdijk, William Ruddock Primett, Ksenia Mukhina, Elen Lotman, Pia Tikka · pp. 90-120
    摘要

    Empirical studies on cinematographers’ creative processes remain limited, likely due to theoretical gaps. Drawing from the embodied mind theory, we hypothesize that cinematographers in action holistically simulate the characters’ situatedness in the diegetic world and, in doing so, translate it into the future viewers’ experience. We report initial findings from semi-structured interviews with cinematographers after shooting a dramatic scene with a handheld camera. Two main approaches were identified in the interview data related to participating cinematographers’ embodied decision-making processes. We describe detailed themes identified within each of these two approaches and adopt image schematic structures as a provisional explanation of how the cinematographers’ consideration of the actors and viewers’ experience is reflected in their creative decision-making processes. We envision future research building on these findings.

  1. Color, Culture, and Filmmaking: A Comparative Analysis of “The Body” and Its Cross-Cultural Remakes
    Jaime López-Díez, Víctor Suárez-Paniagua, Beatriz González, Pedro Antonio García López, Manuel Melgosa · pp. 1-23
    摘要

    This study examines the cross-cultural use of color in cinema by analyzing El cuerpo (2012) and three remakes from India (Bollywood and South Indian) and South Korea. Using quantitative methods, it compares Hue, Saturation, and Value to explore how filmmakers adapt color to local aesthetics while preserving the narrative. Results show that Hue—especially red tones—varies most, with the South Indian version differing most from the original. Saturation and Value show fewer differences. These findings support theories of cultural proximity and hybridization, suggesting that color is a key stylistic element in cultural adaptation. This study offers a novel approach to analyzing remakes as culturally mediated texts and highlights the value of visual style as an area of divergence in global cinema.

  2. Social Interaction and Embarrassment in the Films by Ruben Östlund
    Birger Langkjær · pp. 1-20
    摘要

    A characteristic of Ruben Östlund’s films is their focus on social behavior and interaction at moments when social scripts are disrupted by instinctual behavior or conflicting concerns. Such disruptions create dilemmas within a given social situation, most often leading to embarrassment. The article examines the nature of (comic) embarrassment as the dominant social emotion in Östlund’s films, typically arising from a contrast between a desired projection of the self and one’s actual behavior within a social setting. Often, though, the durative observational style provides secondary characters and the audience with forms of vicarious embarrassment not necessarily shared by the main characters. It is argued that Östlund utilizes embarrassment in both playful and transgressive ways, which prompts ambiguity about how viewers may react to and interpret his films, but also has the potential to be a cinema of engagement.

  1. Theatres of feminism and cinema’s vanished ladies: Dame May Whitty and Margaret Lockwood beyond the narrow gaze of Hitchcock Studies
    J E Smyth · pp. 233-257 · online 2026-09-30
    摘要

    In designating the male director-auteur the key to determining a film’s meaning and cultural value, film history and criticism have systematically devalued the impact of women working in the industry in other roles. Since the 1960s and the rise of academic film studies, Alfred Hitchcock has become emblematic of the field’s creative and gendered hierarchy, but among all the films he worked on, The Lady Vanishes (1938) is the one that he and many critics struggled to brand as the unquestionable property of the ‘Master of Suspense’. Claire Warden and Diana Taylor have argued that performance studies can successfully challenge 'the disciplinary compartmentalisation of the arts'; in this essay, focusing on actresses Dame May Whitty and Margaret Lockwood rather than on director Alfred Hitchcock is intended to revive conversations between women in theatre and film history, histories of feminist advocacy and the erasure of women on and off screen. The Lady Vanishes is a site of feminist history, renewal and possibility; in this essay it acts a two-way mirror, reflecting back upon Whitty’s legendary theatrical career and gazing forward to Lockwood’s rejection from the post-war British film industry and return to a life in the theatre. Ultimately, retracing the careers of The Lady Vanishes’ two female stars offers something beyond a feminist reconsideration of one of British cinema’s most beloved films. Whitty’s and Lockwood’s working experiences, spanning British entertainment history from the late 19th century through to the 1970s, offer a sobering comparison between women’s work in theatre and film under the growing shadow of male executives, critics and ‘star’ directors.

Social Media + Society

Vol. 12, No. 4 · 2026-10 · 8 篇 · SAGE · Media
  1. Algorithms of Injustice: An Ethnographic Content Analysis of Black Youth’s Experiences with Racial Trauma Online
    Amanda M. McLeroy, Tiera Tanksley · online 2026-10-13
    摘要

    This study explores the pervasive presence of racially traumatic content (RTC) on social media and its profound impact on the mental well-being of Black youth. Utilizing ethnographic content analysis and grounded in critical race technology theory (CRTT), the research identifies key manifestations of RTC, including Black death, digital harassment, deepfakes, and war content. Social media’s algorithmic structures amplify RTC, perpetuating systemic anti-Black racism while exposing Black youth to vicarious trauma, anxiety, and depression. Despite these challenges, social media platforms also provide spaces for activism and community-building. Black youth use these digital arenas to engage in social justice narratives, find solidarity, and develop coping strategies. The findings emphasize the critical roles of educators and counselors in mitigating the adverse effects of RTC by fostering media literacy, digital resilience, and culturally responsive mental health support. This research contributes to evidence-based interventions aimed at empowering Black youth to navigate digital spaces safely, advocate for systemic change, and thrive in an equitable and inclusive online environment.

  2. Automated Influence: How Social Bots Reshape Discursive Opportunities in Online Activism
    Linda Li, Helen Margetts · online 2026-10-15
    摘要

    Social bots are an increasingly prominent presence in online protest discourse. We adapt the discursive opportunity structure (DOS) framework for digital protest, refining its dimensions of visibility, resonance and legitimacy to account for platform-mediated discourse and automated activity. Applying the refined framework through computer-assisted content analysis of bot activity in two major protests – Black Lives Matter (BLM) and Extinction Rebellion (XR) – we move beyond treating bots as a monolithic or uniformly disruptive force, identifying competing pro- and counter-protest strategies distributed differently across the three dimensions. In BLM, pro-protest bot content concentrates on cultural resonance, whereas counter-bot content targets legitimacy; in XR, pro-protest bots are used to build legitimacy and participatory visibility, whereas counter-bots more often work to downplay the movement’s salience. The framework’s granularity discriminates between movement types in ways coarser accounts of ‘political bots’ cannot, showing that understanding contemporary movements requires attending to how automated activity is deployed within their discourse.

  3. Careful Dis/Connection: Bringing Digital (Inter)Dependent Relations Out From the Shadows of Autonomy
    Sara Van Bruyssel · online 2026-10-13
    摘要

    Although always-on connectivity affords autonomy, it also creates dependencies on digital devices. Digital disconnection has emerged as a countermovement, with people seeking ‘authentic’ offline reconnection under the discourse of (self-)care. Yet, a pertinent question is whether certain care relations are contingent on digital dependencies between people and, despite being perceived as burdensome, should therefore not be broken through disconnection. Drawing on feminist ethics of care, which understands care as all the work that maintains everyday life, this article examines how care and digital dis/connection practices shape each other. Empirically, it draws on ethnographic fieldwork with 20 adults and analyses three vignettes through a relational framework that follows care occurring in, through and away from digital devices. The findings show that digital well-being is shaped less by individual media use than by the care relations that digital technologies sustain. Experiences of digital dis/connection reveal how connectivity is embedded in often invisible responsibilities, dependencies, and forms of care. Rather than locating digital tensions in technologies themselves, the analysis suggests that such tensions expose underlying challenges in how care is organised, valued, and distributed. In response, the article develops the framework of careful dis/connection to evaluate digital practices according to how they sustain, redistribute, and make visible the care on which everyday life depends.

  4. Do Experts Agree About Misinformation? Evidence From Three Annual Surveys
    Sacha Altay, Sebastián Valenzuela, Philip N. Howard · online 2026-10-25
    摘要

    How much do experts on the information environment agree about the scale, spread, and effects of misinformation? We drew on three annual waves of the International Panel on the Information Environment expert survey to examine agreement with seven popular claims about misinformation (2023: N = 262; 2024: N = 413; 2025: N = 442; spanning 76 countries). Agreement varied widely across statements and was stable across waves. About 60% of experts agreed that falsehoods spread faster than the truth and that misinformation is widespread. About half agreed that misinformation has a substantial influence on behavior and that people readily believe what they see online; about 40% agreed that more people have become misinformed over time; and only 25% agreed that misinformation is primarily a social media problem. The surveys also revealed a persistent divide between experts who saw misinformation as widespread and consequential and those more skeptical, with sharpest disagreement on its behavioral impact and increase over time. In 2025, some experts distinguished between misperception prevalence and misinformation severity. Overall agreement varied by respondent background: agreement was higher among experts on developing countries than those on developed countries, higher among STEM researchers than social scientists, and lower among political scientists than communication scholars. Expert consensus on misinformation is partial, varies across claims and experts, and is shaped by geography and discipline.

  5. Exploring the Predictors and Outcomes of Perceived Algorithm Responsiveness in Social Media Feeds: Combining Longitudinal and Computational Methods
    Samuel Hardman Taylor, David M. Markowitz · online 2026-10-25
    摘要

    Perceptions of how algorithms curate content are integral to how people use social media and its effects. Building on the algorithm responsiveness process, we explore whether perceived algorithm responsiveness (PAR) was associated with the message cues of algorithmically curated content. We coupled longitudinal data from a 7-day, twice-daily diary survey with computational digital traces from Twitter (now X) to operationalize algorithmic message characteristics and to understand the antecedents and outcomes of PAR ( N = 10,337 tweets). Results suggested that message cues from algorithmic content in social feeds (i.e., closeness, popularity, valence, and format) were associated with perceived message personalization, but message personalization was the only message characteristic associated with PAR. PAR predicted feeling more connected to others on Twitter, along with message-level factors: closeness to the poster and message personalization. This work contributes to the algorithm responsiveness process, demonstrating that characteristics of algorithmically curated messages predict how individuals experience algorithms.

  6. Seeing Is Believing? Most People Struggle to Detect AI-Generated Images
    Mads Fuglsang Hove, Frederik Hjorth, Yevgeniy Golovchenko, Rebecca Adler-Nissen, Anja Bechmann, Arjen van Dalen et al. · online 2026-10-08
    摘要

    Generative artificial intelligence (AI) dramatically lowers the costs of creating high-quality, realistic images, allowing a wider range of actors to create and distribute such content on, for instance, social media. A core concern is that easy-to-use generative AI tools can be misused for manipulation through misinformation and microtargeted persuasive material. However, we still need more contextual nuance to a fundamental question: To what degree can people discern photorealistic AI-generated images from authentic images? In a preregistered survey experiment, we find that, on average, people are not substantially better than chance at discerning AI-generated images from authentic images, regardless of the context of the image. While younger people, highly analytical-thinking individuals, and experienced users of generative AI have better discernment abilities, no demographic group other than young individuals (<25) performs substantially better than chance. Contrary to our expectation, we do not find that people’s confidence in their discernment abilities varies depending on the context of the image. As generative AI technology likely advances and the realism of AI-generated images continues to improve, these findings are concerning, as people already struggle to discern between AI-generated and authentic images, even in an experiment where they have been primed and are assessing images of relatively low quality.

  7. Social Media Platform Repertoires in Everyday Life: Measuring Social Media Use With Passive Metering
    Moreno Mancosu, Mario Quaranta, Fabio Torreggiani · online 2026-10-17
    摘要

    Social media platforms structure the ways in which billions of citizens access information. Nevertheless, to assess social media time use, most research relies on limited data sources, such as self-reported survey measures or behavioral data from single platforms or convenience samples. Using 6 weeks of passive metering of smartphone and computer activity from a quota sample of Italian adults (18–70, n = 1,457), this study provides a behaviorally measured account of platform-specific social media use across the adult population. We quantify time allocation across Facebook, Instagram, TikTok, YouTube, and Twitter/X and examine how use varies by sociodemographic factors. Results show that Facebook and YouTube dominate everyday social media time, accounting for nearly three-quarters of consumption, while Instagram attracts comparable average time from a narrower user base, and TikTok shows concentrated usage among younger adopters. Twitter/X occupies a marginal niche. Use is overwhelmingly app-based (>90%), a pattern that browser-based measurement cannot capture. Age is the strongest demographic predictor: Gen Z concentrates attention on Instagram and TikTok, while older cohorts favor Facebook; YouTube shows weak demographic patterning. Gender and education are consistent secondary predictors: women devote more time to Facebook, Instagram, and social media overall, and higher education is associated with lower aggregate use and a smaller share on Facebook and TikTok. Employment and income show null effects. Together, these behavioral, cross-platform figures provide a more accurate basis for weighting platforms by their actual audiences than self-reported or single-platform evidence allows.

  8. Webs of Ainu Mosir: Indigenous Creative Networks on YouTube
    Xanat Vargas Meza, Takumi Yokoyama, Chieko Nishimura, Ryohei Nishida, Yoichi Ochiai · online 2026-10-13
    摘要

    The Ainu are Indigenous people living primarily in Japan. As they have recently adopted social media, and network-based analyses of First Nations cultural manifestations are uncommon, this study drew on Indigenous and social network frameworks to examine creative networks in the virtual Mosir (the world as viewed by the Ainu). Our objective was to clarify the types of collaborators, their cooperation patterns and relationships relevant to producing Ainu-related YouTube videos. Three YouTube lists of 428 videos were utilised in this study. Publicly available data were downloaded using a YouTube Application Programming Interface (API). The videos were watched and classified, including anonymised data on collaborators. The relationships of the collaborators were quantified and mapped, network measurements were calculated and collaborator clusters were examined. The most frequent types of collaborators were individuals and communities. We also observed that individuals moderately affected network clustering ( p = .002). Furthermore, individuals were somewhat correlated with the average number of ties ( p = .004) and the bridging of clusters through betweenness ( p = .006), with Japanese male celebrities and Ainu people, particularly Ainu women, showing high centrality. As Indigenous rights advance internationally, the virtual Mosir shows how crucial it is for First Nations to participate in multimedia production processes, particularly if such productions are available on social media. Indigenous network frameworks were highly compatible with network analysis, allowing for a more comprehensive overview of Indigenous production processes. This can enhance the multiple knowledges of First Nations, while fostering accountability and reciprocity of their allies.

Studies in Documentary Film

Vol. 20, No. 3 · 2026-09-02 · 7 篇 · Taylor & Francis · Film
  1. The screen of fantasy: ethics of witnessing death in Afternoons of Solitude
    Mustafa Uzuner · pp. 141-153 · online 2026-01-02
    摘要

    This essay examines Albert Serra's Afternoons of Solitude (2024) through a critical framework informed by André Bazin's realist ethics, Serge Daney's reflections on cinematic risk, and Vivian Sobchack's phenomenology of the ethical gaze. Grounded in Bazin's conception of the screen as a sacred threshold and Daney's critique of its sacrificial function, the analysis explores how Serra's formal austerity-characterized by long takes, distant framings, and durational stillness-at once evokes Bazinian ideals of temporal integrity and undercuts the ethical vulnerability they imply. The camera's unflinching gaze, rather than signaling moral courage, registers as aesthetic detachment. Drawing on Michel Leiris's evolving disillusionment with bullfighting as a form of ‘violent spectacle,’ and engaging the metaphysical provocations of Georges Bataille and Giorgio Agamben, I argue that Serra's cinema enacts a refusal of rupture. By rendering death as a motif rather than a singular event, his film transforms the spectacle of killing into an aesthetic loop, insulating both filmmaker and viewer from consequence. In contrast to the animal's unmediated encounter with the ‘open’ and the ritual's demand for corporeal risk, cinema here becomes a fraught intermediary-capable of bearing witness only when it reckons with its own limits as medium and moral frame.

  2. Haptic sound for trauma storytelling in a cinematic virtual reality poetic documentary – The Road to Yesterday
    Ningning Song · pp. 154-168 · online 2026-03-17
    摘要

    Cinematic Virtual Reality (CVR) documentaries offer new possibilities for narrating trauma, yet their narrative grammar remains underdeveloped. This article examines how haptic sound supports trauma storytelling in my autobiographical CVR practice, The Road to Yesterday (2025). Drawing on Marks's concept of haptic perception, I develop a haptic sound framework grounded in the materiality of sound, low-frequency resonance, spatialised ambience and vibration-based haptics to explore how haptic sound can evoke embodied access to traumatic memory beyond linguistic or cognitive representation. I further argue that, within the documentary context, animated re-enactment and sound design beyond realism do not replicate reality but constitute an inquiry into reality and trauma. These nonrepresentational strategies offer an aesthetic buffer that creates distance from forced identity substitution or overstating “experiencing the other,” thereby reducing the risk of improper witnessing. The Road to Yesterday positions the immersant as a proximate third-person witness, maintaining psychological autonomy while enabling ethical witnessing. The findings suggest that multisensory approaches, particularly haptic sound, may help mediate traumatic experience and open a potential therapeutic space. In contexts where mourning rituals are disrupted, such as pandemic-era bereavement, CVR can facilitate symbolic closure and sustain continuing bonds through sensory and imaginative engagement.

  3. Dust lung: suffering, spectatorship, and the ethics of witnessing in Chinese independent documentary culture
    Wenxi Hu · pp. 169-185 · online 2026-04-06
    摘要

    This paper examines how contemporary Chinese independent documentaries represent pneumoconiosis workers and their entanglement with dust, labor, and disposability. Drawing on Zygmunt Bauman’s concept of “wasted lives,” alongside theories of slow violence and transcorporeality, it argues these films function as counter-visual practices that not only document structural neglect but also reframe the ethical and political value of marginalized lives in the public sphere. The first section explores how documentaries construct a “dust landscape,” a visual ecology of industrial decay in which environmental degradation blurs the boundary between laboring bodies and ruined environments. Here, dust operates both as toxic material and as a metaphor for the erasure of vulnerable workers from developmentalist narratives of progress. The second section turns to the affective and embodied dimensions of illness, analyzing how pneumoconiosis patients are portrayed within intimate familial settings. These representations move beyond spectacle, showing how suffering is lived and negotiated within precarious kinship structures. The final section examines the ethics of circulation and reception, highlighting how digital platforms, participatory practices, and audience responses shape meaning. By situating filmmaker, subject, and viewer in a triangulated relation of visibility, these documentaries challenge dominant logics of productivity and open space for revaluing life through care, memory, and resistance.

  4. Whose disaster is this? Petroextractivism, visual sovereignty, and the documentary representations in Assam’s 2020 Baghjan oil blowout
    Anusuya A. Paul · pp. 186-205 · online 2026-05-11
    摘要

    The 2020 Baghjan oil blowout in Assam in which a gas well operated by Oil India Limited burned uncontrolled for 173 days, devastating the Maguri-Motapung wetland and displacing the indigenous Moran community was not a natural disaster but the structural consequence of a century of petroextractivism applied to areas surrounded by indigenous communities in postcolonial India’s northeast. This paper argues that the blowout produced not one catastrophe but two: the material devastation of ecological destruction and indigenous dispossession, and the epistemological catastrophe of its misrepresentation. Through a comparative analysis of Inside the Burning (2021) and Baghjan Reels (2022), the paper demonstrates that these films construct fundamentally antagonistic realities of the same event. Drawing on documentary theory, semiotic analysis, catastrophe aesthetics, counter-visuality, and decolonial epistemology, it reveals how Inside the Burning deploys classical narrative conventions to transform systemic governance failure into a corporate epic of heroic resolution, while Baghjan Reels enacts a decolonial counter-visuality constituting the Moran community not as objects of knowledge but as its primary producers. The paper argues that in petroextractivist frontiers like Assam, environmental justice is inseparable from epistemic justice, and the right to represent disaster is the right to define harm.

  5. Collage or chaos? Music documentary and the art of audiovisual remediation in Moonage Daydream
    Lisa Perrott · pp. 206-234 · online 2026-06-03
  6. Fragments of fear: smartphone testimonies and the ethics of witnessing in #Nova
    Shmulik Duvdevani · pp. 235-251 · online 2026-07-03
    摘要

    The October 7, 2023 massacre at the Nova Music Festival near the Gaza border in southern Israel was unprecedented not only in its scale and brutality, but also in the way it was documented. For the first time, a terrorist attack was filmed simultaneously by both victims and perpetrators: festivalgoers recorded their flight and hiding through smartphone cameras, while Hamas militants documented the assault through body-mounted GoPro cameras. #Nova, composed of videos, voice messages, and WhatsApp recordings, reconstructs the attack in real time. Drawing on theories of found-footage horror cinema, media witnessing, and documentary gazes, this article examines two contrasting modes of looking: a “prophylactic gaze,” associated with the victims' recordings as a form of psychological survival, and a “violent gaze,” embodied in the perpetrators' footage. Situating #Nova alongside The Blair Witch Project, the article argues that the film blurs the boundary between fictional found-footage horror conventions and the audiovisual documentation of real violence, redefining spectatorship in the age of smartphone testimony and digitally mediated terror.

  7. Dreamlike reverie through Maria Casares’s voice-over in the 1950 documentary Guernica, directed by Alain Resnais and Robert Hessens
    Ludovico Longhi, Alba Anaya Domínguez · pp. 252-268 · online 2026-07-27
    摘要

    This article analyzes the 1950 documentary Guernica, directed by Alain Resnais and Robert Hessens. Through close reading that integrates film analysis and sound studies, it examines how visual, narrative, and auditory elements combine to construct an impression of reverie and hypnotic absorption in the viewer. Central to this effect is the voice-over performance of actress María Casares, whose delivery of Paul Éluard’s text contributes to transforming the film into a poetic, affective interpretation of historical trauma. Moving beyond an objective account of the Spanish Civil War, the film fuses Picasso’s artworks, Éluard’s poetry, and sound design to construct a mediated memory of the bombing of Guernica. The article argues that the film offers an early instance of what Josep M. Català has termed the ‘oneiric mode’ in documentary, positioning Guernica both in relation to the essay film tradition and as a significant work for sensory and affective approaches to historical representation.

Television & New Media

Vol. 27, No. 8 · 2026-12 · 7 篇 · SAGE · TV/Media
  1. TV POWWW! and Televised Play in the Early Videogame Industry
    Tom Boellstorff, Braxton Soderman · pp. 743-761 · online 2026-01-30
    摘要

    TV POWWW! was a television program built around a technology which allowed viewers to control televised videogames over the telephone. This article chronicles its history while analyzing its technology, social contexts, aesthetics, and impact on game design and development. We situate TV POWWW! at the nexus of several developments during the late 1970s and early 1980s. These include new perceptions of mediated interaction facilitated by computerization and technical communication networks, experiments in participatory television, ideas about simplified videogame design for televised play contexts, and forms of gendered spectatorship and play.

  2. Interface Games as Microgenre: Definition and Thematic Investigation
    Finn Dawson, Cassandra Barkman · pp. 762-783 · online 2026-01-07
    摘要

    In this article, we identify and define an emerging microgenre of videogame we call the interface game. While all videogames contain interfaces, “interface games” as a microgenre refers to videogames that remediate user interfaces such as desktops and mobile screens, making entire game worlds that are oriented around interacting with these interfaces. Interface games are underexplored and often come out of independent production. Through close textual analysis, we analyze three broad thematic preoccupations in interface games—surveillance, nostalgia and digital identity—and show how they interrogate the underlying sociality of digital interfaces in contemporary capitalism. We demonstrate how videogames are uniquely positioned to question the aesthetic, social, interactive, political and playful dimensions of user interfaces, and their significant role in our everyday lives. In doing so, we show how novel approaches to videogame microgenres are necessary to fully understand the variety of contemporary games production.

  3. Glocalizing Queer Caricature: RuPaul’s Drag Race UK and the Snatch Game Format
    Hannah Andrews · pp. 784-800 · online 2026-01-30
    摘要

    RuPaul’s Drag Race is viewed by many as responsible for popularizing the art of drag around the world. Remakes have appeared in diverse national settings, including Australia, Denmark, the Philippines, and the UK. Formats have been seen as evidence of growing globalization in the television industry. The concept of “glocalization,” a portmanteau term developed in the 1980s to signify the modification of products and services to suit local audiences, is a more appropriate framework through which to view TV formatting. This article considers the combination of glocalization with queer caricature (drag comic impersonation) via a regular feature of the Drag Race formula: the “Snatch Game” in which drag artists compete by impersonating celebrities. Taking RuPaul’s Drag Race UK as its main case study, it explores how Snatch Game performances evidence differing layers of “glocalization,” in its parody of figures from the local/national to the transnational.

  4. Here Comes the Future: Black Women and Early Digital Culture in Essence
    Myrna Moretti · pp. 801-818 · online 2026-01-22
    摘要

    This article evaluates the articles and columns that covered the emergence of computers and other personal electronics in the Black women’s magazine Essence during the 1980s and early 1990s. Through a close reading of how habits and time management were repeatedly used as a framing device, this article demonstrates the tension between neoliberal and Black feminist perspectives across the coverage of technology in the magazine. This article positions attitudes about emerging technology alongside larger social and cultural contexts for Black American women by identifying how the discourse authored by Black women evince multivalent temporalities and a collective voice. Evaluating women’s magazines for their gendered and raced attitudes on technology emphasizes a non-specialist and everyday discourse history that politicizes histories of electronics and digital technology. This paper contributes to a growing body of scholarship that elaborates the development of Black women’s digital culture.

  5. Beyond Gatekeeping: Web Series as Entrepreneurial Emancipation in the Contemporary Streaming Era
    Sarah de Possesse, Brian Yecies, Steinar Ellingsen, Sue Turnbull · pp. 819-837 · online 2025-12-01
    摘要

    This study explores fictional web series as a disruptive alternative to traditional television industry gatekeeping systems, offering actor/producers unprecedented pathways to creative agency and entrepreneurial career development. This research documents how practitioners, industry bodies, broadcasters, and audiences define “successful” web series, examining how “value” is generated beyond traditional economic metrics. Drawing on semi-structured interviews with a cross-section of Australian screen industry representatives, it applies an “entrepreneuring as emancipation” framework to reconceptualize the industrial significance of web series as vehicles for challenging established hierarchies. Finally, it analyzes how the low-budget format enables actor/producers to develop entrepreneurial identities through digital platforms while maintaining full control over creative, productive, and dissemination processes. By examining web series as tools that prioritize non-economic metrics of success, this study reveals impactful forms of career advancement, demonstrating how web series continue to transform screen industries through democratized content creation and distribution for actor/producers globally.

  6. Welcome (back) to Hogwarts: Re-adaptation, intertextuality, and cultural disputes in the Harry Potter reboot
    Guillermo Echauri · pp. 838-854 · online 2025-11-24
    摘要

    Harry Potter stands as one of the most culturally and commercially significant media franchises. The original seven-book literary saga was successfully adapted into a film series that cemented the franchise’s global impact. Currently, a new streaming television series based on the same source material is in production. Drawing on early promotional discourses—corporate statements, official social media posts, and verified production leaks—this article examines the narrative, industrial, and cultural implications of this reboot. Conceptualized as a re-adaptation, the series employs narrative retelling as a strategy for franchise renewal and expansion. In doing so, it activates multiple layers of intertextuality among the literary source, the classic films, and the upcoming television series. This reinforces broader processes of artistic, cultural, and media hybridization. Simultaneously, the reboot unfolds amid cultural disputes, positioning itself as a safe commercial and cultural investment while inevitably engaging with tensions surrounding issues such as race and authorship.

The Velvet Light Trap

    Mattie Jacobs · pp. 1-2
  2. “Stay Prayed Up”: Mark Wahlberg, Participatory Culture, and the Future of Stardom in the Post-Cinematic Media Regime
    Stephen Spanellis · pp. 3-12
    摘要

    Mark Wahlberg is more meme than man. This article offers an analysis of Wahlberg’s star-produced texts that attempt to curate the image of a God-fearing star with an obsessive work ethic and the satirical fan-produced text “Mark Wahlberg’s Daily Routine” that sees through Wahlberg’s attempt to obfuscate his past. Wahlberg offers an instructive case study when interrogating the future of stardom. In the ever-changing post-cinematic media landscape, if stardom in the studio sense has died, it has been reincarnated as something new, an intertextual collaborative process of decentralized authorship, networked memes, and dialogic (if sometimes antagonistic) play between celebrity and fan.

  3. The Hybrid Auteur: Steven Soderbergh, The Knick , and the Reinvention of Television Authorship
    R. Colin Tait · pp. 13-28
    摘要

    This essay argues that The Knick (Cinemax, 2014–2015) provides a rare case study of how creative authority and production labor were reorganized during the height of Peak TV. Instead of viewing Steven Soderbergh’s direction, cinematography, and editing of all twenty episodes as evidence of auteur dominance, the article presents the series as a hybrid director-showrunner model shaped by the industrial context that made it possible. Drawing on original interviews with Soderbergh and cocreator Jack Amiel, the analysis demonstrates how The Knick ’s fast pace, unified leadership, and platform-specific factors at Cinemax redistributed creative responsibilities across writing, direction, and postproduction in ways that differed from typical US television practices. Building on scholarship on production cultures, narrative complexity, and television legitimation, the article shows that The Knick ’s praised visual coherence came directly from its labor structures and time limitations. The article asks how Soderbergh’s multirole involvement reorganized production labor and schedule on The Knick and what this reveals about the possibilities and limits of director-centered authorship in modern US prestige television. By placing the series within its industrial context, the article reinterprets The Knick as a production experiment where creativity and coherence stemmed from an unusual combination of economic factors, collaborative practices, and directorial approach.

  4. “I’m a Mutt”: VTubing and Performing Race on the Liquid Internet
    Rachel Diane Williams, Collin Hawley · pp. 29-41
    摘要

    VTubing, or live-streaming using a virtual avatar rather than one’s own face, allows content creators to maintain a level of privacy that is not typically afforded to streamers. Further, the semi-anonymity of VTubing allows creators from communities that are often marginalized and harassed in digital spaces to mitigate some of the more abusive treatment they experience. This essay challenges postracial and colorblind popular internet discourses, arguing that in these digital spaces there remains persistent racism, bigotry, and misogyny, and that race simultaneously always risks melting into incoherence when the constructed nature of identity categories comes into focus. Analyzing the content of the VTuber Kenji, this essay examines how his complex articulation of his hybrid identity, refusing reductions of that identity, serves to exemplify the liquefaction of racial structures in digital spaces.

  5. Modding Menstruation
    Dana Villareal · pp. 42-52
    摘要

    This essay is centered around an investigation into modifications to The Sims 4 that represent menstruation. It takes a critical look at the potential for destigmatizing menstruation through this kind of representation. Two mods to The Sims 4 are examined: KawaiiStacie’s “Slice of Life Mod” and turbodriver’s “WonderfulWhims.” This analysis is buttressed with autoethnographic reflection of gameplay of these mods. I suggest that affirmative and normative representation of menstruation in life-simulation gameplay contributes to destigmatization. I also outline steps for modders to further accurately reflect menstruation experiences in lifestyle-simulation modifications.

  6. Obama’s Talking Heads: Lip-Sync Technologies, Deepfakes, and Fiction and Nonfiction Media
    Amy Skjerseth · pp. 53-63
    摘要

    This article situates deepfakes in a long history of lip-syncing, where technicians use racial and gendered stereotypes to heighten spectators’ belief in synchronized speech. Inspired by technicians who call their deepfakes “talking heads,” I compare deepfakes to the talking-head device in documentary film. Talking-head deepfakes—like Jordan Peele’s 2018 PSA deepfake of Obama—caricature a person’s voice to create a believable persona. Thus, the talking-head deepfake carries lip sync’s technological and aesthetic baggage of synchronizing a subject to a stereotypical image. In all, deepfakes reflect social and political concerns that also have permeated documentary and audiovisual studies.
